Implementation of `rfft_convolve(a, b)`:
```
def fft_convolve(a,b):
    n = len(a)+len(b)-1
    N = 1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:n]
```
Then `smooth_gradient(a, w)`:
```
w_rev = w[::-1]
conv = fft_convolve(a, w_rev)
corr = conv[::-1]? We need c_j = conv[L-1-j] for j=0..n-1. With L=2n-1, indices L-1 down to 0. So c = conv[:n][::-1]? Wait L-1=2n-2. j=0 -> index 2n-2; j=n-1 -> index n-1? Actually L-1-j ranges 2n-2 down to n-1. conv length 3n-2. So c = conv[n-1:2n-1][::-1]? Let's check n=2: conv indices 0..3; L-1=2; j=0 index2; j=1 index1. That's conv[1:3][::-1] = [conv2,conv1]. For general, start index n-1? j=0 index 2n-2=2(n-1); j=n-1 index n-1. So slice [n-1:2n-1] length n reversed. `c = conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]`. ```

For top-k direct gradient:
```
def topk_gradient(a, peak_indices, weights):
   n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
   for k,w in zip(peak_indices,weights):
      if w<... continue
      js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
      if je<js continue
      js_idx=np.arange(js,je+1)
      g[js:je+1] += 2*w*a[k-js_idx]
```
This is O(K n). Could be faster for K=20. But all-weights FFT may capture broad peaks and avoid artificial top-k. We can use both.

Let's design helpers:
- `fast_convolve(a)`: FFT linear convolution. Ensure real, small negative clip? Return length 2n-1. For many calls, returning arrays. Use `np.maximum(conv,0)`? Conv of nonnegative should be nonnegative but FFT roundoff small negative. Clipping may slightly affect objective but okay. However for gradient exact, we use weights from clipped conv. Fine.
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n=a.size
    size=2*n-1
    N=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:size]
    # clip small negative
    if C.min() < 0: C=np.maximum(C,0.0)
    return C
```
- `normalize(a)`: returns nonnegative sum1. If sum<tiny, uniform.
- `symmetrize(a)`: 0.5*(a+a[::-1]); normalize.
- `interpolate_seq(f, new_n)` as before.
- `make_power_left(n, alpha, eps=1.0)`: a=np.power(np.arange(n)+eps, -alpha); normalize. For alpha close 0.5.
- `make_power_right similarly`?
- `make_power_both(n, alpha, beta, eps)`: a=( (i+eps)^-alpha + (n-1-i+eps)^-beta ), but if alpha+beta>0.5 maybe center blow; still finite for n small. We can include α,β combos.
- `make_gaussian`, `make_bell`, `make_bimodal`, `make_uniform`.
- `make_mixture(f1, weight, f2)`.
- `evaluate_shape(a)`: uses fast_convolve and returns score = 2*n*maxc (since normalized sum=1). This is same as evaluate_sequence for normalized nonnegative sum1 within float. But for final verification use evaluate_sequence. Use shape score for speed (no list conversion). Could have small differences due to clamp? no.

Detailed smooth_mirror:
```
def smooth_mirror(a0, time_limit, p_start=2, p_max=128, eta_init=1.0, topk=0, verbose=False, label=""):
    a = normalize(np.asarray(a0,dtype=np.float64))
    n = a.size
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    M = c.max()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_M = M
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    p = p_start
    p_target = p_max
    eta = eta_init
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = 0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        it += 1
        c = fast_convolve(a)
        M = c.max()
        # maybe if M very low break? no
        # weights
        r = c / (M + 1e-300)
        # r in [0,1]
        if p == 1.0:
            w = r
        else:
            w = np.power(r, p)
        sw = w.sum()
        if sw < 1e-300:
            w = np.ones_like(r) / len(r)
        else:
            w = w / sw
        if topk > 0:
            K=min(topk,len(c)); idx=np.argpartition(c,-K)[-K:]; # reweight only top idx? Could use w topk already if p large. Keep all.
        g = strong_gradient(a, w) # choose all FFT
        gc = g - g.mean()
        # line search
        gnorm = np.sqrt(np.dot(gc,gc)) + 1e-15
        base = eta / gnorm
        # Choose best trial among factors
        chosen = None; chosen_M = M*(1+1e-12); chosen_score = M
        for fac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02):
            step = base*fac
            lu = -step*gc
            if lu.max()>50 or lu.min()< -50: lu=np.clip(lu,-50,50)
            cand = a*np.exp(lu)
            cand = normalize(cand)
            cc = fast_convolve(cand)
            cm = cc.max()
            # Accept if reduces max; also if reduces Jp
            if cm < chosen_M - 1e-14:
                chosen=cand; chosen_M=cm
                if cm < M*(1-1e-6): break
        if chosen is None:
            # try smaller fixed?
            chosen = a.copy()
        # update a with chosen? If chosen_M >= M, using a that didn't reduce may still improve smoothed? We can still take if it reduces weighted sum?
        # compute chosen weighted objective maybe
        # For simplicity, if chosen_M < M, take; else reduce eta and maybe take if no_improve < few? Need progress.
        if chosen_M < best_M - 1e-13:
            best_M = chosen_M; best_a = chosen.copy()
        if chosen_M < M - 1e-13:
            a = chosen
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            # If no improvement, try additive small? Or break? Reduce p/eta.
            # Use a = chosen if chosen_M <= M*(1+1e-6) to continue? Could oscillate.
            if chosen_M <= M * (1 + 1e-7):
                a = chosen
            no_improve += 1
            eta *= 0.5
        if no_improve > 50:
            p = min(p * 1.2, p_max * 2)
            if p > p_max*2: p = p_start
            eta = eta_init * 0.5
            if no_improve > 200:
                # perturb best
                rng = np.random.default_rng(1000 + it)
                noise = rng.standard_normal(n)
                a = best_a + noise * (0.001 + 0.002*min(no_improve/1000,1))
                a = normalize(np.maximum(a,0))
                p = p_start
                eta = eta_init
                no_improve = 0
        # symmetrize periodically
        if it % 100 == 0:
            a_sym = symmetrize(a)
            c_sym=fast_convolve(a_sym); M_sym=c_sym.max()
            if M_sym < best_M - 1e-13:
                best_M=M_sym; best_a=a_sym.copy()
            if M_sym < M:
                a=a_sym.copy(); c=c_sym; M=M_sym; no_improve=0
        if verbose and it % 100 == 0:
            print(f"{label} it={it} M={M:.10f} best={best_M:.10f} p={p:.2f}")
    return best_a, 2*best_a.size*best_M
```
But line search only accepts if max reduces. In nonconvex landscape, small max-reducing steps may be rare; smoothing p might require steps that temporarily increase max to escape local. We can allow acceptance if smoothed objective Jp decreases. Compute `Jp = np.sum(np.power(c/(M+eps), p))`? Since weights normalized, objective H_p=σ? We can compute weighted objective `H = np.sum(w_flat * c)` (where w_flat maybe r^p). For line search, accept if `H_trial < H*0.999` or max improves. But if p large, H_trial decrease might still increase max? Could happen. But final best tracked. Use both:
- Current `H = np.dot(w, c)` (sum c^p / sum c^{p}? Actually w normalized). This scales.
- Trial `Ht = np.dot(w, cc)`? Weights from current c, not trial. For linear approximation.
Accept if trial M < current M, or trial H < H*(1-1e-6). If accepted but M increases, we can move a but not update best. This allows escaping. But if M increases too much, might be bad. Cap: require M <= best_M *1.05? maybe.
Better accept if `chosen_M < M` OR (`chosen_M < best_M * 1.02` and `Ht < H * (1-1e-4)`). Then set a to chosen. But if M gets too high, sym/restart later.

Need compute weights and gradient exactly. For p-smooth, weights `w = r^p`. If p large, w concentrated top. The gradient of J_p (without normalization) would be p r^{p-1}; but using normalized weights changes step by factor p*M? We normalize w sum1; gradient of max surrogate ∑ w c with w fixed; okay.

Additive descent:
```
def additive_mirror(a0, time_limit, p_start=2, p_max=128, dt_init=0.05, verbose=False, label=""):
   a=normalize(a0); n; best...
   it=0; dt=dt_init; p=p_start; no_improve=0
   while time<limit:
      c=fast_convolve(a); M=c.max(); r=c/M; w=r**p; normalize w
      g=strong_gradient(a,w); gc=g-g.mean()
      gnorm = sqrt(dot(gc,gc))+eps
      base=dt/gnorm
      for fac in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
         cand= project_simplex(a - base*fac*gc)
         cm=...
         accept similar
      update p, dt, sym, restart.
```
Projection helper `project_simplex` from previous. Need ensure returns nonnegative sum1. For large n, O(n log n) okay.

def peak_refine(a0, time_limit, verbose=False, label=""):
   a=normalize(a0); n; best=...
   while time:
      c=conv; top_k = min(6,len(c)); peak_idx=argpartition(c,-top_k)[-top_k:]
      w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[peak_idx]=1/top_k
      g=strong_gradient(a,w); # gradient of active peak average
      # Try additive and multiplicative moves with small steps in direction -g
      improved=False
      for step_type in ['mul','add']:
         for frac in [0.1,0.05,0.02]:
            if mul: gc=g*g.mean(); cand=normalize(a*exp(-frac*gc?)) but step scale.
```

Coordinate descent final might be useful. Let's implement a simple **random coordinate multiplier heuristic** with vectorized small steps:
```
def coordinate_refine(a0, time_limit, verbose=False, label=""):
    a=normalize(a0); n; best_M; best_a
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    while time:
       # pick random 32 coordinates (or all if n small) ordered by current a maybe top? High mass coordinates more likely improve.
       if n <= 1024:
           idxs=np.arange(n)
       else:
           # weighted sample by a
           idxs=np.unique(rng.choice(n, size=min(64,n), replace=False, p=a))
       improved=False
       for j in idxs:
          old=a[j]
          if old < 1e-8: continue
          # Try scaling old by factors and distributing to rest proportional to a
          for mult in (0.7,0.9,1.1,1.3):
             new=old*mult
             if new<0 or new>=1: continue
             cand=a.copy()
             cand[j]=new
             diff=old-new
             if diff>0: redistribute to positions not j proportional to a? If increasing, subtract from rest proportional.
             rest_mask=np.arange(n)!=j
             rest_sum=a[rest_mask].sum()
             if diff>0:
                 cand[rest_mask] *= (1 - diff/rest_sum) # maintain sum? old sum rest, new sum rest = rest_sum - diff, scale factor (1-diff/rest_sum)
             else:
                 cand[rest_mask] *= (1 - diff/rest_sum) # diff negative => scale up by (1+|diff|/rest_sum)
             cand=np.maximum(cand,0); cand/=cand.sum()
             if fast_convolve(cand).max()<best_M-1e-14:
                a=cand; best_M=...; best_a=a.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved: possibly continue to reduce? Could try adding tiny mass to zero coords.
    return best_a,best_score
```
But this changes sum by scaling rest, can make negative if diff>rest_sum? mult up to 1.3 => diff=0.3old, rest_sum≥1-old; if old small ok; if old near1 impossible. Check factor positive: 1 - diff/rest_sum >0. For increasing old (diff negative), factor >1. For decreasing, diff positive; require diff<rest_sum. If old>0.5 and mult<1? diff up to0.3old<0.15<0.5 rest maybe okay. Use safe.
This coordinate method similar previous but random. It uses FFT for each mult (4-6). For n=768, 64*4=256 FFT per round, ~0.1-0.2s? Actually each FFT 2048? 256*0.001=0.25s. Could run many. But if n=4096, too slow. Limit n<=1536 or time 30s.

Maybe use **batch coordinate** by computing new c formula to avoid FFT for many j? Too complex.

- `a_i ∝ (i+ε)^-α * ( (n-i+ε)/n )^-β?` Maybe.

If γ>0, f(1-t) near t=0 has (1-t)^-γ/2 ~1, so okay. At t=1, f(t) ~ (1-t)^-γ/2, f(1-t)~ (1-t)^-0.5 => exponent (γ+1)/2 at both endpoints of x=1 integral; need <1? Actually integrability at t=1 requires γ/2 + 1/2 <1? Wait product at t=1: f(t) ~ (1-t)^-γ/2, f(1-t) ~ (1-t)^-0.5 -> exponent (γ+1)/2. Integral finite if <1 => γ<1. So can add right endpoint exponent up to <0.5? Maybe improves max by balancing. Score formula for f(t)=t^{-1/2}(1-t)^{-γ/2}. Normalize. At x=1, g=C² ∫ t^{-1/2}(1-t)^{-γ/2} * [(1-t)^{-1/2} t^{-γ/2}]? =C² ∫ t^{-(1+γ)/2}(1-t)^{-(1+γ)/2} dt = C² B((1-γ)/2,(1-γ)/2), finite if γ<1. Norm integral B(1/2,1-γ/2). g= B((1-γ)/2,(1-γ)/2) / B(1/2,1-γ/2)^2. For γ=0: B(1/2,1/2)/B(1/2,1)^2 =π/4. For γ=0.5: B(0.375,0.375)/B(0.5,0.75)^2. B(0.375^2)? Gamma0.375 ~? One-sided only left; center peak contributions are pairs t and 1-t; both left endpoint (t small) and right endpoint of f (t near1) contribute. To reduce center, reduce either left or right endpoint density. But reducing left endpoint also reduces x small? For one-sided, f near 0 singular. If we taper f near 1 (right endpoint) to zero, center contributions from t near1 reduce? But t near1 in f(t) corresponds to f(1-t) near0? Wait center x=1 integral over t∈[0,1] f(t)f(1-t). If f has singular only at 0, f(1-t) singular at t=1. So center peak arises from left endpoint f(t) and right endpoint f(1-t). If we taper right endpoint to 0, center integral may reduce, but then edge x near2 (sum near2) low; not a problem? Max might be elsewhere. This could yield lower max! One-sided f has full singular at right because f(1-t) uses f near 1? But if f one-sided only at 0, f near1 is not singular (t^-1/2 near1 is finite 1). Wait f(t)=t^-0.5; f(1-t) near t=1 = (1-t)^-0.5 singular. So the singular at t=1 comes from the same left endpoint of f evaluated at 1-t, not f near 1. To taper right endpoint f near1 does not affect f(1-t) near t=1? It is f(1-t) = f(small), singular. So cannot taper right endpoint to reduce center; the singular is left endpoint of f, not right. The center peak is convolution of two left endpoints (X near0,Y near1 => Y=1-t near0? Actually f(1-t) near t=1 small argument). It requires distribution mass near 0; any mass with sum near1 can have one variable near0 and the other near1? Wait if f only near0, how can variable near1? f(1-t) small argument means Y=1-t near0, so X near1, Y near0. But f(x) near1 is finite (not mass), yet to have X near1 with finite density, contribution ∫ f(X)f(Y) maybe? The Bessel integral with both singular at 0 for X=0,Y small? Hmm for sum near1, pairs X≈t,Y≈1-t; f(t) singular if t small, f(1-t) singular if 1-t small (t near1). But f is not singular near1, but f(1-t) as function of t has singular at t=1 because argument is small (1-t). So center peak is from one variable near0 and the other near1? If distribution f places negligible mass near1, how can one variable near1? It does place finite density near1 (value ~0.5), enough with singular near0 to yield integral π/4? Actually ∫0^1 t^-0.5 *1 dt finite but not π? The π comes from both endpoints: f(t) singular near0 and f(1-t) singular near1 (Y small). That requires X near1 and Y near0? But X near1 has density finite, not singular; the singular f(1-t) near t=1 is Y=1-t small, and X=t near1 finite. Product ~ (1-t)^-0.5 * const, integral finite but O(1)? Hmm one-sided integral ∫0^1 t^-0.5 (1-t)^-0.5 =π/4. The integrand is singular at both t=0 and t=1. At t=0: X small singular, Y≈1 finite. At t=1: X≈1 finite, Y small singular. So both pairs: (small, near1) and (near1, small). Finite density near1 gets multiplied by singular near0 for all near1 values? The integral near t=1 ∫(1-t)^-0.5 dt =2, finite. So yes. To reduce center, reduce mass near0, but then edge? If reduce left endpoint exponent, score increases. So one-sided may be optimal in one-parameter.

Could use distribution with mass near both 0 and 1 but exponents <0.5? We saw symmetric worsens. What about mass near0 exponent 0.5 and mass near1 exponent 0.1? Sum exponents at center =0.6 >0.5? Wait f(t)=t^-0.5 + (1-t)^-0.1. At x=1, f(1-t) = (1-t)^-0.5 + t^-0.1. Product has term t^-0.5 * t^-0.1 = t^-0.6 integrable (since <1), and (1-t)^-0.5*t^-0.1 integrable? At t=1, (1-t)^-0.5*(1-t)^-0.1=(1-t)^-0.6 integrable. Sum exponents 0.6<1 integrable. This may be finite and perhaps lower max? General add two endpoint singularities with exponents α,β such that α+β<1 for integrability at x=1. For one-sided α=0.5,β=0 gives α+β=0.5. Maybe α=0.4,β=0.4 sum 0.8 finite but may lower max? Detailed helper implementations:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def normalize_nonnegative(v, floor=1e-18):
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if v.size==0:
       return np.array([1.0])
    v=np.clip(v,0.0,None)
    s=v.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-300:
       return np.ones(v.size)/v.size
    v=v/s
    return v
```
Maybe don't floor here.

`fast_convolve`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    size=2*n-1
    N=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:size]
    # remove tiny negative
    if C.size and C.min() < 0.0:
        C=np.maximum(C,0.0)
    return C
```
For n=1, size=1, N=1; bit_length? (0).bit_length()=0, 1<<0=1. ok.

`score_normalized(a)`:
```
def score_normalized(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   if a.size==0: return np.inf
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: return np.inf
   a=a/s
   c=fast_convolve(a)
   return 2.0*a.size*float(c.max())
```
If a has negative? normalize clips.

`exact_score(a)` calls evaluate_sequence; but only if available. Since evaluate_sequence imported. Use in final.
```
def exact_score(a):
    try:
       return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a]))
    except Exception:
       return score_normalized(a)
```

`symmetrize(a)`:
```
def symmetrize(a):
   a=normalize_nonnegative(a)
   return normalize_nonnegative(0.5*(a+a[::-1]))
```

`interpolate_seq(f, new_n)`:
```
def interpolate_seq(f, new_n):
   f=np.asarray(f,float).ravel()
   old_n=f.size
   if old_n==new_n: return normalize_nonnegative(f)
   x0=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old_n)
   x1=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
   if old_n==1:
      val=np.full(new_n, f[0]); ...
   newv=np.interp(x1,x0,f)
   return normalize_nonnegative(newv)
```
But if f is not sampled over 0..1? okay.

`make_uniform(n)`: np.ones(n)/n.

`make_power_left(n, alpha=0.5, eps=1.0)`:
```
x=np.arange(n)+eps
a=x ** (-alpha)
return normalize_nonnegative(a)
```
If alpha=0, uniform.

`make_power_both(n, alpha=0.5, beta=0.0, eps=1.0)`:
```
xL=np.arange(n)+eps
xR=n-1-np.arange(n)+eps
a=xL**(-alpha)+xR**(-beta)
return normalize
```
Maybe if beta=0 xR^0=1; okay.

`make_gaussian(n, center=0.5, sigma=0.2)`:
```
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
a=np.exp(-0.5*((x-center)/sigma)**2)
return normalize
```

`make_bell(n, width=0.6)`:
```
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
t=np.clip(np.abs(x-0.5)/(width/2),0,1)
a=(1-t**4)
return normalize
```

`make_mixture(a,b,w)`: normalize(w*a+(1-w)*b). Need if lengths differ? use same n.

`project_simplex(v)`: as previous.
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).ravel()
    n=v.size
    if n==1: return np.array([max(float(v[0]),0.0)])
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    cond=u*np.arange(1,n+1) > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
       return np.ones(n)/n
    rho=np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1]
    theta=(cssv[rho]-1.0)/(rho+1.0)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
This projects onto sum=1 if v sum maybe not 1? Standard. If v has negatives? okay.

`strong_gradient(a, w)`: as above, handle n=1.
```
def strong_gradient(a,w):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); w=np.asarray(w,float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==1:
       return np.array([2.0*a[0]*w[0]])
    L=2*n-1
    if w.size != L:
       # pad/trunc? shouldn't
       tmp=np.zeros(L); m=min(L,w.size); tmp[:m]=w[:m]; w=tmp
    needed = n + L -1
    N = 1 << (needed-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    W=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)[:needed]
    start=n-1
    stop=2*n-1
    if stop>conv.size: stop=conv.size
    corr=conv[start:stop][::-1]
    if corr.size < n:
       tmp=np.zeros(n); m=min(n,corr.size); tmp[:m]=corr[:m]; corr=tmp
    return 2.0*corr
```
Test stop: for n=2, stop=3, conv size=4, ok. For n=1, handled.
Potential Python memory: w[::-1] view, rfft okay.

`top_weights(c,p)`:
```
def exponent_weights(c, p):
   c=np.asarray(c,float).ravel()
   M=c.max()
   if M <= 0: return np.ones(c.size)/c.size
   r=c/M
   if p <= 1.0:
       w=r
   else:
       w=np.power(r,p)
   sw=w.sum()
   if sw<1e-300 or not np.isfinite(sw):
       # fallback top ones
       idx=np.argmax(c); w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1.0
   else:
       w=w/sw
   return w
```
For p large, underflow; if sw 0, fallback uniform? Better fallback top index. But if top weight only, gradient direct? strong_gradient with one w okay.

`make_trial_multiplicative(a, gc, step)`:
```
def make_mul_candidate(a, gc, step):
   lu=-step*gc
   lu=np.clip(lu,-50,50)
   cand=a*np.exp(lu)
   cand=np.maximum(cand,1e-300)
   s=cand.sum()
   if s<1e-300: return a.copy()
   return cand/s
```
`make_add_candidate(a, gc, step)` project_simplex.

Optimization function `smooth_mirror` design. Need pass `verbose` and `label`. It uses loops, no closures.
Pseudo:
```
def smooth_mirror(a0, time_limit, p_start=2.0, p_max=128.0, eta_init=1.0, verbose=False, label=""):
    a=normalize_nonnegative(a0)
    n=a.size
    if time_limit <= 0:
       return a, score_normalized(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    c=fast_convolve(a); M=float(c.max())
    best_a=a.copy(); best_M=M
    p=p_start; eta=eta_init; no_improve=0; it=0
    # optional warmup best score
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        it+=1
        c=fast_convolve(a); M=float(c.max())
        if M <= best_M:
            if M < best_M - 1e-15:
                best_M=M; best_a=a.copy()
            no_improve = 0? maybe
        else:
            no_improve += 1? But M can fluct.
        w=exponent_weights(c,p)
        g=strong_gradient(a,w)
        gc=g-g.mean()
        gnorm=float(np.sqrt(np.dot(gc,gc)))
        if gnorm < 1e-15:
            # stationary, perturb
            a=symmetrize(a); ...
        base=eta / gnorm
        base=min(base, 2.0)
        chosen=None; chosen_M=M; chosen_H=None; H=float(np.dot(w,c))
        for fac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02):
            step=base*fac
            if step <=0: continue
            cand=make_mul_candidate(a,gc,step)
            cc=fast_convolve(cand); cm=float(cc.max())
            # Weighted objective using current w
            ch=float(np.dot(w,cc))
            # acceptance criterion
            if cm < M*(1-1e-14):
                break accept
            if ch < H*(1-1e-6) and cm < best_M*1.02:
                break accept
        if chosen not None:
           M=chosen_M; a=chosen; c=chosen_c
           if M < best_M - 1e-14: update best; no_improve=0
           else: no_improve+=1? If moved without max improvement, set no_improve=0? To allow escape, maybe reset small if H decreased.
        else:
           # no accepting trial: reduce eta, maybe do tiny step if H decreased? Keep a; no_improve++
           eta *= 0.5
           no_improve += 1
        # adapt p
        if no_improve > 30:
            p = min(p * 1.15, p_max*4)
            if p > p_max*4:
                p = p_start
            eta = eta_init * 0.5
            if no_improve > 150:
                # perturb best
                rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + it)
                scale = 0.001 + 0.003*min(no_improve/1000,1.0)
                a = best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale
                a = normalize_nonnegative(np.maximum(a,0.0))
                p = p_start
                eta = eta_init
                no_improve = 0
        # sym every 75?
        if it % 75 == 0:
            a_sym=symmetrize(a)
            c_sym=fast_convolve(a_sym); Msym=float(c_sym.max())
            if Msym < best_M - 1e-14:
                best_M=Msym; best_a=a_sym.copy()
            if Msym < M:
                a=a_sym.copy(); c=c_sym; M=Msym; no_improve=max(0,no_improve-10)
        if verbose and it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"[{label}] it={it} M={M:.10f} best={best_M:.10f} p={p:.2f} eta={eta:.3e}")
    return best_a, 2.0*best_a.size*best_M
```
Acceptance: need implement carefully without inner function. We'll set `trial_accepted=False` and break.
For H objective, `dot(w, cc)` with normalized w. If p high and current top peak, H decreases when top weights reduce. Good.

Potential issue: Multiplicative update with gradient centering: For max minimization, if gradient has negative values (weighted correlation nonnegative, centered can negative). Multiplicative update increases where gradient < mean, decreases where > mean. Since autocorr? Good.

Step base min 2.0? If gnorm small, base large but clip log. Maybe min base 1.0 to avoid too much. Use `base = min(eta / gnorm, 1.0)`? If eta=1, base≤1. Good. Multiplicative update with unit step may be strong. Let's set eta_init=0.5, min base 1.0.

When best_A length changes, gnorm maybe? okay.

Coordinate refine:
```
def coordinate_refine(a0, time_limit, max_coords=64, verbose=False,label=""):
   a=normalize_nonnegative(a0); n; best_M; best_a; t0
   rng=np.random.default_rng(987)
   while time<limit:
      if n<=1024:
         idxs=np.arange(n)
      else:
         size=min(max_coords,n)
         # weighted sample; a sums 1
         idxs=np.unique(rng.choice(n,size=size,replace=False,p=a))
      improved=False
      for j in idxs:
         old=float(a[j])
         if old < 1e-8 or old > 0.9: continue
         rest_sum=1.0-old
         if rest_sum < 1e-8: continue
         for mult in (0.7,0.9,1.1,1.3):
             new=old*mult
             if new<0 or new>=1: continue
             diff=old-new
             factor=1.0-diff/rest_sum
             if factor <=0: continue
             cand=a.copy()
             cand[j]=new
             mask=np.ones(n,dtype=bool); mask[j]=False
             cand[mask]=a[mask]*factor
             cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
             ss=cand.sum()
             if ss<=1e-15: continue
             cand/=ss
             cm=fast_convolve(cand).max()
             if cm < best_M - 1e-14:
                a=cand; best_M=cm; best_a=a.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved:
          # try adding small mass to low positions? skip
          # break if n small? maybe continue with random no progress; after many no improve break
          no_improve_global +=1
          if no_improve_global > 5: break
      else:
          no_improve_global=0
   return best_a, 2*best_a.size*best_M
```
But for n=768 and time 100s, one full round (all coords*4 FFT) maybe ~4*768 FFT(2048). Each FFT rfft 2048 ~0.0001s? 3072 FFTs ~0.3s? Actually 3072*0.0002=0.6s. Many rounds. Good. For n=2048, FFT 4096? 4*2048=8192*0.0005=4s/round; 25 rounds in 100s. okay. For n=4096, maybe too slow; limit call if n<=2048.

Maybe `coordinate_refine` can improve final.

Pseudo code with variables:
```
def smooth_mirror(a0, time_limit, p_start=2.0, p_max=64.0, eta_init=0.8, verbose=False, label=""):
    a = normalize_nonnegative(a0)
    n = a.size
    if time_limit <= 0.0:
        return a, score_normalized(a)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_M = M
    p = p_start
    eta = eta_init
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
        it += 1
        c = fast_convolve(a)
        M = float(c.max())
        if M < best_M - 1e-14:
            best_M = M
            best_a = a.copy()
        w = exponent_weights(c, p)
        g = strong_gradient(a, w)
        gc = g - float(np.mean(g))
        gnorm = float(np.sqrt(np.dot(gc, gc)))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            # perturbation
            rng = np.random.default_rng(777 + it)
            a = normalize_nonnegative(np.maximum(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.001, 0.0))
            continue
        base = eta / gnorm
        if base > 1.0:
            base = 1.0
        H = float(np.dot(w, c))
        chosen = None
        chosen_M = M
        chosen_c = None
        accepted = False
        for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05):
            step = base * fac
            if step <= 0.0:
                continue
            lu = -step * gc
            lu = np.clip(lu, -50.0, 50.0)
            cand = a * np.exp(lu)
            cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-300)
            ss = float(cand.sum())
            if ss <= 0.0:
                continue
            cand = cand / ss
            cc = fast_convolve(cand)
            cm = float(cc.max())
            ch = float(np.dot(w, cc))
            if cm < M - 1e-15 or (ch < H - 1e-12 and cm < best_M * 1.05):
                chosen = cand
                chosen_M = cm
                chosen_c = cc
                accepted = True
                if cm < M - 1e-6:
                    break
        if accepted and chosen is not None:
            a = chosen
            c = chosen_c
            M = chosen_M
            if M < best_M - 1e-14:
                best_M = M
                best_a = a.copy()
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve = min(no_improve + 1, 1000)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            eta *= 0.5
        if no_improve > 40:
            p = p * 1.2
            if p > p_max * 4.0:
                p = p_start * 0.5
            eta = eta_init * 0.5
            if no_improve > 180:
                rng = np.random.default_rng(999 + it)
                scale = 0.001 + 0.004 * min(float(no_improve) / 2000.0, 1.0)
                a = best_a + rng.standard_normal(n) * scale
                a = normalize_nonnegative(np.maximum(a, 0.0))
                p = p_start
                eta = eta_init
                no_improve = 0
        if it % 100 == 0:
            a_sym = symmetrize(a)
            c_sym = fast_convolve(a_sym)
            Msym = float(c_sym.max())
            if Msym < best_M - 1e-14:
                best_M = Msym
                best_a = a_sym.copy()
            if Msym < M:
                a = a_sym.copy()
                c = c_sym
                M = Msym
                if no_improve > 0:
                    no_improve -= 10
        if verbose and it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"[smooth {label}] it={it} M={M:.10f} best={best_M:.10f} p={p:.2f} eta={eta:.3e}")
    return best_a, 2.0 * float(best_a.size) * best_M
```
One issue: If accepted move increases M but decreases H, `no_improve` increments and may cause p increase, but a may oscillate. The condition `cm < best_M * 1.05` prevents too high. Good.

Another idea: Since objective is max coefficient of A^2, we can use **Newton's method for flat polynomial**: We want A^2 close to target T(z)=1/(2n-1) ∑ z^k plus spectral adjustments. Given current A, choose correction Δ such that (A+Δ)^2 ≈ T. Linearize: 2AΔ ≈ T - A^2. Solve Toeplitz equation 2AΔ = r. Since A is Toeplitz? Multiplication by A(z) is convolution, so solve Δ = (r / A^2?) in frequency: Δ_hat = r_hat / (2 A_hat). Then project Δ to real, set A_new=A+Δ, renormalize, ensure nonnegative. This is Newton in polynomial square. Could flatten coefficients faster. But A_hat may have zeros, and Δ may make A negative. We can use damped and sym. Let's explore.

Given c = A^2. Target T maybe uniform (1/L) plus perhaps spectral feasible. Residual r = T - c. We want update A in direction d such that coefficients of 2A d approximate r. In Fourier on large circle, D = R / (2 F) where F=A_hat, R=FFT(r). Then d = IFFT(D). Use damped A_new = A + λ d. This resembles Newton for square root of target spectrum. If choose T flat, A should approximate sqrt(flat spectrum). But sqrt of flat discrete-time Fourier may not yield nonnegative coefficients. Still, iterative may find good.

We can implement `newton_flatten(a, target)`:
- Target could be current c smoothed? To reduce max, set T_k = 1/L for all k? But A^2 sum=1, uniform T sum=1. Need A maybe with negative? We can compute d and accept if max improves.
- Or target T = c but with peaks flattened: T_k = min(c_k, M_target) and renormalize? To guide.
This could be a powerful global step. Let's derive discrete polynomial square exact? A(z) length n. Squared polynomial length 2n-1. We can solve for d length n such that A_new = A + d, and A_new^2 ≈ T (truncate to length 2n-1). Linear equation for d: 2*A*d = r (linear convolution truncated). This is a linear convolution system; in FFT, D = R/(2F) but convolution in frequency works if no aliasing and if we solve infinite? For finite, still approximate. Use large FFT size N≥2*(n+?)-1. Let's implement simpler `newton_flatten(a0, time_limit, verbose)`:
```
def newton_flatten(a0, time_limit, target_func='flat', verbose=False):
   a=normalize(a0); n; best...
   while time:
      c=fast_convolve(a); M=c.max()
      # target uniform with maybe edge weighting? T = 1/(2n-1)
      T = np.full(2*n-1, 1.0/(2*n-1))
      # But pure uniform may not be achievable; maybe target = 0.5*c +0.5*uniform? To be closer.
      T = 0.5*c + 0.5*uniform
      r = T - c
      N=1 << (3*n-3 -1).bit_length() # for A*d length?
      A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
      R=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
      denom=2*A
      # avoid zeros: denom = denom + eps*sign? Use np.where(abs>eps, denom, eps)
      D = R / np.where(np.abs(denom)>1e-12, denom, 1e-12)
      d = np.fft.irfft(D,N)[:n]
      # real part; also consider -d? Since Newton direction.
      for lam in [0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02]:
          cand=normalize(a + lam*d) (clip)
          if max improves accept
          # also try a - lam*d? maybe not
      if no improve, perturb.
```
But D division by A where A has zeros can huge. Clip d to [-10,10]. Since A positive on unit? For real nonnegative polynomial, A(z) may vanish on unit? Yes possible. Need robust. This might not help. But could be an extra phase 100s.

Could implement **Adam optimizer** manually in log-domain. Adam may handle nonconvex better. Variables y=log(a) unconstrained; update a=normalize(exp(y)). Gradient wrt y = gradient*scale? For objective f(a), da/dy = a⊗I - a a^T. So grad_y = a * grad_f - (a*grad_f).sum()*a. Adam with adaptive learning rates. Could be more robust than mirror. But many variables. We can implement `adam_log(a0, time_limit, p)`:
- y = log(a+eps).
- m,v arrays.
- Loop: c, w, grad_f = 2corr(w,a). grad_y = a*grad_f - a*dot(a,grad_f). Update Adam.
- Compute new a = softmax(y). Evaluate max. Maybe line search/backtrack: if max worsens, reduce alpha.
Adam can escape plateaus and has per-coordinate steps, perhaps better. But nonconvex. We can include as another method.

Implementing Adam top-level:
```
def adam_log(a0, time_limit, p_start=2, p_max=64, alpha_init=0.01, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, eps=1e-8, verbose=False,label=""):
   a=normalize(a0); n; y=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-15))
   m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
   best...
   t=0
   while time:
      t+=1
      a=np.exp(y-y.max()); a=a/a.sum()
      c=conv; M; update best
      w=exponent_weights(c,p)
      gf=strong_gradient(a,w)
      gy=a*gf - a*float(np.dot(a,gf))
      m=beta1*m+(1-beta1)*gy; v=beta2*v+(1-beta2)*gy*gy
      mhat=m/(1-beta1**t); vhat=v/(1-beta2**t)
      step=alpha_init/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
      # clip step
      y_new=y-step
      # normalize not needed
      a_new=softmax(y_new)
      M_new=conv(a_new).max()
      # accept if M_new < M or weighted H decreased
      if accept: y=y_new; ... else reduce alpha
      adapt p
```
However if we y update with no line search and max worsens, can revert but keep m/v? Standard Adam always updates; for nonconvex may be okay. Use `alpha` adaptive: if M_new > M*1.02, alpha*=0.5 and skip; else keep. This is stochastic? deterministic.
Momentum in y could cause stagnation. But maybe good.

Let's write propose flow:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)
    best_a=None; best_score=inf
    # parent load
    par = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    parent=None
    if par is not None: ...
    if parent is not None:
       sc=score_normalized(parent)
       if finite: best...
    # lengths
    lengths=[768,1024,1536,2048,3072,4096]
    if parent is not None:
       pl=parent.size
       if pl<=8192: lengths.append(int(pl))
       if pl>4096 and pl<=8192: lengths.append(8192)
    lengths.append(8192)
    # unique sorted
    unique_lengths=[]
    for L in lengths:
       L=int(L)
       if L<2 or L>8192: continue
       if L not in unique_lengths: unique_lengths.append(L)
    unique_lengths.sort()  # no lambda
    # If too many >6, maybe filter: keep those closest to parent and powers
    # 7 lengths ok.
    top_candidates=[]
    for n in unique_lengths:
        seqs=[]
        if parent is not None:
           p_n=interpolate_seq(parent,n); seqs.append(p_n); seqs.append(symmetrize(p_n))
        # maybe if n==parent.size, parent already
        for alpha in (0.48,0.5):
           seqs.append(make_power_left(n,alpha))
        seqs.append(make_gaussian(n,0.5,0.18))
        seqs.append(make_bell(n,0.55))
        m=make_mixture(make_power_left(n,0.5), make_gaussian(n,0.5,0.25),0.85)
        seqs.append(m)
        for seq in seqs:
           if seq.size != n: continue
           sc=score_normalized(seq)
           if np.isfinite(sc):
              if sc < best_score:
                  best_score=sc; best_a=seq.copy()
              # top list
              if len(top_candidates) < 8:
                  top_candidates.append([sc, seq.copy()])
              else:
                  max_idx=0; max_sc=top_candidates[0][0]
                  for idx in range(1,len(top_candidates)):
                      if top_candidates[idx][0] > max_sc:
                          max_sc=top_candidates[idx][0]; max_idx=idx
                  if sc < top_candidates[max_idx][0]:
                      top_candidates[max_idx]=[sc, seq.copy()]
    # Ensure best_a not None
    if best_a is None:
       best_a = make_power_left(1024,0.5); best_score=score_normalized(best_a)
    # main start selection
    main_a=best_a.copy(); main_len=best_a.size
    for item in top_candidates:
       sc, a = item
       if sc <= best_score + 0.010 and a.size > main_len:
           main_a=a.copy(); main_len=a.size
    # Also consider sym of main_a
    a_sym=symmetrize(main_a); sc_sym=score_normalized(a_sym)
    if sc_sym < best_score - 1e-12:
       best_score=sc_sym; best_a=a_sym.copy()
    if sc_sym < score_normalized(main_a): main_a=a_sym.copy()
```
But if `main_a` from longer top has score slightly worse but will be optimized. Good.

    if rem>120 and best_a.size < 8192:
       targets=[]
       bl=best_a.size
       for t in [1536,2048,3072,4096,8192]:

`direct_gradient` implementation as previous with factor 2. For each idx, slice length up to n. If idx list length 64,n=4096, 256k, fine. Use vectorized slice: `g[js:je+1] += 2.0 * wi * a[je:js-1:-1]?` Need reversed. For js..je, k - idx = [k-js, ..., k-je], which is reversed segment of a from k-js down to k-je. ```
def direct_gradient(a, indices, weights):
    n=a.size; g=np.zeros(n)
    for i in range(len(indices)):
       k=int(indices[i]); wi=float(weights[i])
       if wi <=0: continue
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       if je<js: continue
       idx=np.arange(js,je+1)
       g[js:je+1] += 2.0*wi*a[k-idx]
    return g
```
Good.

Implement `make_mul_candidate(a, gc, step)`:
```
def make_mul_candidate(a, gc, step):
    lu = -step * gc
    lu = np.clip(lu, -50.0, 50.0)
    cand = a * np.exp(lu)
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-300)
    ss = float(cand.sum())
    if ss <= 1e-300:
        return a.copy()
    return cand / ss
```

`make_add_candidate(a, gc, step)`:
```
def make_add_candidate(a, gc, step):
    cand = a - step * gc
    cand = project_simplex(cand)
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-15)
    ss=float(cand.sum())
    if ss>0: cand=cand/ss
    return cand
```

def newton_flatten(a0, time_limit, verbose=False,label=""):
  a=normalize; best...
  L=2n-1; uniform=np.full(L,1/L)
  while time:
    c=fast_convolve(a); M=c.max()
    r=uniform - c
    N=1<<(3*n-3 -1).bit_length() # enough for A*d maybe
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); R=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
    denom=2*A
    # divide safely
    D = np.zeros_like(A)
    mask=np.abs(denom)>1e-12
    D[mask]=R[mask]/denom[mask]
    d=np.fft.irfft(D,N)[:n]
    # clip d to avoid huge: scale d by n maybe
    d_max=np.max(np.abs(d)); if d_max>0: d=d/(d_max) * max(0.1, 1.0/n*10?)
```

Let's implement stable `newton_flatten`:
```
def newton_flatten(a0, time_limit, reg_frac=1e-8, max_disp=0.1, verbose=False, label=""):
    a=normalize_nonnegative(a0); n; L=2n-1; uniform=np.full(L,1.0/L)
    t0=...
    while time:
       c=fast_convolve(a); M=...
       r=uniform - c
       needed = 2*(n+n-1)-1? For A*d where A length n,d length n, conv length 2n-1. But division created d length N; no need A*d separately. For FFT solving, N should be >=2n-1 to avoid circular convolution alias in A*d? If we use D = R/(2A) in DFT, A*d cyclic convolution length N. Need N >= 2n-1 to represent linear A*d. But r length 2n-1. So N >= 2n-1 (not 3n). Since A and d both n, product length 2n-1. Use N=nextpow2(2n-1), same as conv. Good.
       N=1<<(2*n-2 -1).bit_length()? For 2n-1 size, N=1<<(size-1).bit_length.
       A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); R=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
       denom=2*A
       denom_abs_sq=np.abs(denom)**2
       reg = reg_frac * np.max(denom_abs_sq) + 1e-30
       D = R * np.conj(denom) / (denom_abs_sq + reg)
       d = np.fft.irfft(D,N)[:n]
       # Scale d to reasonable: d_mean? remove? We can just clip.
       scale = max_disp * max(float(np.max(a)), 1.0/n)
       d = np.clip(d, -scale, scale)
       # Optionally center d? Not, since A update independent, normalize after.
       for lam in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
          cand = a + lam*d; cand=normalize_nonnegative(cand)
          Mnew=conv.max()
          if Mnew < M -1e-14: accept; break
       if no accept, maybe try -d? or reduce max_disp/reg; no_improve++
```

Need define `make_power_left_decay` helper.
```
def make_power_left_decay(n, alpha=0.5, gamma=0.0, eps=1.0):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    x=i+eps
    if n>1: pos=i/(n-1)
    else: pos=np.zeros(n)
    a=x**(-alpha)*np.exp(-gamma*pos)
    return normalize_nonnegative(a)
```
If gamma large, exp underflow okay.

def consider_candidate(seq, best_score, best_a):
    if seq is None: return best_score,best_a
    a=np.asarray(seq,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0 or not np.isfinite(a).all(): return best_score,best_a
    if a.sum() <=1e-10: return...
    sc=score_normalized(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
       return sc, normalize_nonnegative(a)
    return best_score,best_a
```

Top-level helper:
```
def update_best(seq, best_score, best_a):
    if seq is None:
        return best_score, best_a
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return best_score, best_a
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.sum() <= 1e-12:
        return best_score, best_a
    sc = score_normalized(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best_score = float(sc)
        best_a = a / a.sum()
    return best_score, best_a
```
No closure.

```
best_score, best_a = update_best(a_new, best_score, best_a)
best_score, best_a = update_best(symmetrize(a_new), best_score, best_a)
```

def update_best_exact(seq, best_score, best_a):
    if seq is None: ...
    sc = exact_score(seq)

    return best_score,best_a

    final_score = exact_score(final_a)
    # test sym
    sym = symmetrize(final_a)
    sym_score = exact_score(sym)
    if sym_score < final_score: final_a=sym; final_score=sym_score
    # maybe test parent? already in best.
    # ensure float list
    ans = normalize_nonnegative(final_a)
    # final exact of ans?
    ans_score=exact_score(ans)
    if ans_score < final_score: final_score=ans_score
    print(f"FINAL exact score: {final_score:.6f} n={ans.size}")
    return [float(x) for x in ans]
```

Code:
```
def newton_flatten(a0, time_limit, reg_frac=1e-6, verbose=False, label=""):
    a = normalize_nonnegative(a0)
    n = a.size
    if time_limit <=0 or n==0: return a, score_normalized(a)
    L=2*n-1
    uniform = np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    c=fast_convolve(a); M=float(c.max()); best_a=a.copy(); best_M=M
    it=0; no_improve=0; lam_scale=0.5
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       it+=1
       c=fast_convolve(a); M=float(c.max())
       if M < best_M -1e-14: best_M=M; best_a=a.copy()
       r = uniform - c
       size=2*n-1
       N=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
       A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       R=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
       denom=2.0*A
       denom_abs2=np.abs(denom)**2
       reg = reg_frac * float(np.max(denom_abs2)) + 1e-16
       D = R * np.conj(denom) / (denom_abs2 + reg)
       d = np.fft.irfft(D,N)[:n]
       # Clip d relative to a
       a_max = float(np.max(a))
       if not np.isfinite(a_max) or a_max <=0: a_max=1.0/n
       scale = 0.2 * max(a_max, 1.0/n)
       d = np.clip(d, -scale, scale)
       # Also try remove its mean? Newton direction can shift mass; normalize handles.
       improved=False
       for lam in (lam_scale, 0.5*lam_scale, 0.25*lam_scale, 0.1*lam_scale):
           cand = a + lam*d
           cand = normalize_nonnegative(cand)
           cc=fast_convolve(cand); cm=float(cc.max())
           if cm < M - 1e-14:
               a=cand; M=cm; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           # try small negative direction?
           for lam in (0.05*lam_scale, 0.02*lam_scale):
               cand = a - lam*d
               cand=normalize_nonnegative(cand)
               cc=fast_convolve(cand); cm=float(cc.max())
               if cm < M -1e-14:
                  a=cand; M=cm; no_improve=0; break
           if not improved:
               no_improve += 1
               if no_improve > 20:
                   # reduce regularization? or perturb
                   rng=np.random.default_rng(3131+it)
                   a=normalize_nonnegative(np.maximum(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.001,0.0))
                   no_improve=0
       if it % 100 ==0:
           a_sym=symmetrize(a); c_sym=fast_convolve(a_sym); Msym=float(c_sym.max())
           if Msym < best_M -1e-14: best_M=Msym; best_a=a_sym.copy()
           if Msym < M: a=a_sym.copy(); M=Msym
       if verbose and it%100==0: print...
    return best_a, 2*best_a.size*best_M
```

def coordinate_refine(...):
   stall_rounds=0
   while time:
      if n <= 1024:
         idxs = np.arange(n)
      else:
         idxs = np.unique(rng.choice(n,size=32,p=a))
      # add targeted
      c=fast_convolve(a); K=min(8,n*2); peak_idx=np.argpartition(c,-K)[-K:]
      w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[peak_idx]=1.0/K
      g=direct_gradient(a, peak_idx, w[peak_idx])
      gc=g-g.mean()
      if n>1024:
         n_target=32
         top_pos=np.argsort(gc)[-n_target:]
         bot_pos=np.argsort(gc)[:n_target//2]
         random_idx=np.unique(rng.choice(n,size=n_target,replace=False,p=a))
         idxs=np.unique(np.concatenate((top_pos,bot_pos,random_idx)))
      # loop idxs
```

Let's draft code mentally:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def normalize_nonnegative(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if v.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(v.size) / float(v.size)
    return v / s
```
If v all zeros size 1000 -> uniform. That may hide invalid but okay.

`fast_convolve` for n=0? returns [0].
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    size=2*n-1
    N=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:size]
    if C.size:
       mn=float(C.min())
       if mn < 0.0:
          C=np.maximum(C,0.0)
    return C
```
If n=1 size=1, (0).bit_length=0 N=1, rfft length1, irfft length1. ok.

`score_normalized`:
```
def score_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return float('inf')
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <=1e-12: return float('inf')
    a=a/s
    c=fast_convolve(a)
    return 2.0*float(a.size)*float(c.max())
```

`exact_score`:
```
def exact_score(a):
    try:
       a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
       sc=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])
       if np.isfinite(sc): return float(sc)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return score_normalized(a)
```

`project_simplex`: if v.size==0? return empty? But not used. If v all? ok.
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).ravel()
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v
    if n==1: return np.array([max(float(v[0]),0.0)])
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    cond = u * np.arange(1, n+1) > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
       return np.ones(n) / float(n)
    rho = int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / float(rho + 1)
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s=out.sum()
    if s>0: out=out/s
    else: out=np.ones(n)/n
    return out
```
Standard projection assumes target sum 1. If v sum not 1, still projects. Good.

`coordinate_refine`: Implement targeted.
Pseudo:
```
def coordinate_refine(a0,time_limit,verbose=False,label=""):
    a=normalize_nonnegative(a0); n=a.size
    if n==0 or time_limit<=0: return a, score_normalized(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_M=fast_convolve(a).max()
    stall=0; max_stall=20 if n<=1024 else 8
    rng=np.random.default_rng(555)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        idxs = choose...
        improved=False
        # targeted gradient
        if n > 1024:
            c=fast_convolve(a)
            K=min(8, 2*n-1)
            peak_idx=np.argpartition(c,-K)[-K:]
            wtmp=np.zeros(len(c)); wtmp[peak_idx]=1.0/K
            g=direct_gradient(a, peak_idx, wtmp[peak_idx])
            gc=g-g.mean()
            n_t=min(32,n)
            top=np.argsort(gc)[-n_t:]
            bot=np.argsort(gc)[:max(1,n_t//2)]
            rand=np.unique(rng.choice(n, size=n_t, replace=False, p=a))
            idxs=np.unique(np.concatenate((top,bot,rand)))
        else:
            idxs=np.arange(n)
        for j in idxs:
           old=float(a[j])
           if old < 1e-9 or old > 0.95: continue
           rest=1.0-old
           if rest < 1e-8: continue
           for mult in (0.7,0.9,1.1,1.3):
              new=old*mult
              if new <0 or new>=1: continue
              change=new-old
              factor=1.0 - change/rest
              if factor <=0: continue
              cand=a*factor
              cand[j]=new
              cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
              ss=float(cand.sum())
              if ss <=1e-15: continue
              cand=cand/ss
              cm=fast_convolve(cand).max()
              if cm < best_M - 1e-14:
                  a=cand; best_M=cm; best_a=a.copy(); improved=True
                  # don't break; continue with new a other coordinates can improve
        if improved:
           stall=0
        else:
           stall+=1
           if stall > max_stall: break
        if verbose and ...: print
    return best_a, 2*best_a.size*best_M
```
